The tags `…ThreeLineNoSubtitle` and `SwitchListTileThumbImageErrorWithoutImage` are the assertions | generics (§12.7); no Cupertino library |
| `ExpansionTile`, `ExpansionTileTheme`, `ExpansionTileThemeData`, `ExpansibleController`, `Expansible`, `ExpansionTileController` | `NewExpansionTile(title, ExpansionTileOpts{...})`; `Expansible` and `ExpansibleController` are in package `widgets` (Flutter's `widgets/expansible.dart`): `ExpansibleController` embeds a `ChangeNotifier` with `Expand`, `Collapse`, `Toggle` and `IsExpanded`; `ExpansibleControllerOf(bc)` panics with the tag `ExpansibleControllerNoAncestor` and `ExpansibleControllerMaybeOf(bc)` returns nil. `maintainState: false` is the default, so `MaintainState` is a plain `bool` whose zero value is false. **Deviations:** `SemanticsOpts` has no `onTapHint`, so the expand and collapse hints of the header and the `SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement` of the state are not ported; the `ExpansionTileController` of Flutter 3.x is the `ExpansibleController` (Flutter's deprecated alias is not ported) | the options struct convention; no announcements on the desktop |
| `ExpansionPanel`, `ExpansionPanelRadio`, `ExpansionPanelList`, `ExpansionPanelList.radio`, `ExpandIcon`, `MergeableMaterial`, `MaterialSlice`, `MaterialGap`, `MergeableMaterialItem` | `NewExpansionPanel(headerBuilder, body, ExpansionPanelOpts{...})`, `NewExpansionPanelRadio(value any, headerBuilder, body, opts)`, `NewExpansionPanelList(children, ExpansionPanelListOpts{...})` and `NewExpansionPanelListRadio`; `NewExpandIcon(onPressed func(bool), ExpandIconOpts{...})`; `NewMergeableMaterial(children []MergeableMaterialItem, MergeableMaterialOpts{...})` with `NewMaterialSlice(key, child, color...)` and `NewMaterialGap(key, size...)` (an item is a Go interface with `ItemKey()`; Dart's `LocalKey` required for each item stays a `foundation.LocalKey`). The values of the radio panels are `any` compared with `==`. The tags are `ExpansionPanelListDuplicateValues`, `ExpansionPanelListInvalidElevation` and `MergeableMaterialInvalidGaps` (gaps at the ends or next to each other). **Deviations:** the keys of the panel items are `foundation.NewValueKey` values salted with the index (Flutter uses `_SaltedKey`); the `GlobalKey` of each slice of the `MergeableMaterial` is kept in a map by the item's key | the options struct convention; generics are not needed for the values (§12.7) |
| `Divider`, `VerticalDivider`, `DividerTheme`, `DividerThemeData`, `Divider.createBorderSide` | `NewDivider(DividerOpts{...})`, `NewVerticalDivider(VerticalDividerOpts{...})`, `DividerCreateBorderSide(bc, DividerBorderSideOpts{...})` (`bc` may be nil, like Flutter's null context). The tags are `DividerSizeNegative`, `DividerThicknessNegative`, `DividerIndentNegative`, `DividerEndIndentNegative` and the `VerticalDivider…` ones | the options struct convention |
| `Tooltip`, `TooltipTheme`, `TooltipThemeData`, `TooltipVisibility`, `TooltipTriggerMode`, `Tooltip.dismissAllToolTips`, `TooltipState.ensureTooltipVisible` | `NewTooltip(child, TooltipOpts{...})` is built on `widgets.NewRawTooltip` and `painting.PositionDependentBox`; `NewTooltipVisibility(visible, child)` and `TooltipVisibilityOf(bc)` are the inherited switch; `TooltipDismissAll()` and `TooltipState.EnsureTooltipVisible()`. `message` and `richMessage` are both options; the tag `TooltipMessageAndRichMessage` is raised when both are set. `preferBelow` is `Opt[bool]`, `excludeFromSemantics` is `ExcludeFromSemantics`, `enableTapToDismiss: true` is `DisableTapToDismiss`. The tooltip shows when the mouse hovers over the child for `WaitDuration` (none, as in Flutter, unless the options or the theme say another), or on a long press, or on a tap when the trigger mode says so. **Deviations:** the `WaitDuration`, `ShowDuration`, `ExitDuration`, `TriggerMode` and `EnableFeedback` of `TooltipThemeData` are not interpolated by `Lerp` (Flutter does not either) | the options struct convention; negated booleans |
| `Badge`, `Badge.count`, `BadgeTheme`, `BadgeThemeData` | `NewBadge(child, BadgeOpts{...})` (`isLabelVisible: true` is `NoLabelVisible`), `NewBadgeCount(count int, child, BadgeCountOpts{...})` (`maxCount` is `Opt[int]` with the default 999; the tags are `BadgeCountNegative` and `BadgeMaxCountNotPositive`) | the options struct convention |
| `ThemeData.cardTheme`, `dividerTheme`, `tooltipTheme`, `badgeTheme`, `expansionTileTheme`, `listTileTheme` | the six theme data values are `ThemeData` fields, generated with `Lerp`, `Equal` and `CopyWith(…Copy{})` | struct fields |
| `Scrollbar`, `ScrollbarThemeData`, `ScrollbarTheme`, `ThemeData.scrollbarTheme`, `Scrollbar.adaptive` | `NewScrollbar(child, ScrollbarOpts{...})` is a `widgets.RawScrollbar` with Material's thumb and track colors, thickness, radius and hover growth; `ScrollbarThemeData` is the generated theme (`ThemeData.ScrollbarTheme`). `Scrollbar.adaptive` is Material on every platform (Glutter has no Cupertino library). Dart's `_ScrollbarState extends RawScrollbarState` overrides methods that the base calls; Go has no virtual dispatch from an embedded base, so `widgets.RawScrollbarOpts.Subclass` takes a `RawScrollbarSubclass` that the base asks first (`RawScrollbarState.virtual()`) | no virtual dispatch (§12.10 conventions) |
| `LinearProgressIndicator`, `CircularProgressIndicator`, `RefreshProgressIndicator`, `ProgressIndicatorTheme`, `ProgressIndicatorThemeData`, `ThemeData.progressIndicatorTheme` | `NewLinearProgressIndicator(opts)`, `NewCircularProgressIndicator(opts)` (and `…Adaptive`, which is the Material one) and `NewRefreshProgressIndicator(opts)`; `value: null` is `Value: None`; `year2023` defaults to true as in Flutter (`Year2023: Some(false)` for the 2024 look); the painters embed `rendering.CustomPainterBase` | `Opt` for nullable values |
| `RefreshIndicator`, `RefreshIndicator.adaptive`, `RefreshIndicator.noSpinner`, `RefreshCallback`, `RefreshIndicatorStatus`, `RefreshIndicatorTriggerMode` | `NewRefreshIndicator(onRefresh, child, opts)`, `NewRefreshIndicatorAdaptive` (Material), `NewRefreshIndicatorNoSpinner`; `RefreshCallback` is `func() <-chan struct{}` (the refresh is done when the channel is closed or receives), as the Future of Dart; `RefreshIndicatorState.Show` returns a channel that is closed when the refresh is done | Dart `Future` is a channel (§12.10 conventions) |
| `DataTable`, `DataColumn`, `DataRow`, `DataRow.byIndex`, `DataCell`, `DataTableTheme`, `DataTableThemeData`, `TableRowInkWell` | `NewDataTable(columns, rows, opts)`; `DataColumn`, `DataRow` and `DataCell` are value structs made by `NewDataColumn`, `NewDataRow`, `NewDataRowByIndex` and `NewDataCell`; negated booleans for the true defaults (`HideCheckboxColumn` for `showCheckboxColumn`, `SortDescending` for `sortAscending`); `dataRowHeight` (deprecated) is not ported; `OnSelectChanged` takes an `Opt[bool]`. A `DataCell` with `OnDoubleTap` delays its tap, as Flutter's | negated booleans; deprecated API not ported |
| `PaginatedDataTable`, `DataTableSource`, `PaginatedDataTableState`, `AsyncDataTableSource` | `NewPaginatedDataTable(columns, source, opts)`; `DataTableSource` is an interface with the `Listenable` of Flutter's `ChangeNotifier` and `GetRow(i) (DataRow, bool)` (false is Dart's `null`: the row shows a progress indicator); `HideEmptyRows` for `showEmptyRows`; `AvailableRowsPerPage` defaults to 10, 20, 50, 100 | `Opt` and negated booleans |
| `Stepper`, `Step`, `StepState`, `StepStyle`, `StepperType`, `ControlsDetails` | `NewStepper(steps, opts)`, `NewStep(title, content, opts)`; `Step` is a value struct; the horizontal content is kept alive by `NewVisibility(..., VisibilityOpts{MaintainState: true})` | same shapes as Flutter |
| `CarouselView`, `CarouselView.weighted`, `CarouselController`, `CarouselScrollPhysics`, `CarouselViewThemeData`, `CarouselViewTheme`, `ThemeData.carouselViewTheme` | `NewCarouselView(itemExtent, children, opts)`, `NewCarouselViewWeighted`, `NewCarouselViewBuilder`, `NewCarouselViewWeightedBuilder`; `consumeMaxWeight` and `enableSplash` are `NoConsumeMaxWeight` and `DisableSplash`; `AnimateToItem` returns a `*scheduler.TickerFuture`; the render objects override the layout hooks of `RenderSliverFixedExtentBoxAdaptor` through `rendering.FixedExtentLayoutOverrides` (`OverrideIndexToLayoutOffset`, `OverrideMinChildIndexForScrollOffset`, `OverrideMaxChildIndexForScrollOffset`, `OverrideComputeMaxScrollOffset`), which the base asks first, since Go has no virtual dispatch. The tags are `CarouselViewFlexWeightsInvalid`, `CarouselControllerNotAttached`, `CarouselControllerMultipleAttached`, `CarouselScrollPhysicsPosition` and `CarouselPositionExtentOrWeights` | no virtual dispatch; negated booleans |
| `ScrollPositionWithSingleContext.goBallistic` | passes the outermost position (`self`) to `ScrollPhysics.CreateBallisticSimulation`, not the embedded base, so that physics of a custom position type (`CarouselScrollPhysics`) can recognise it | no virtual dispatch |
| `SemanticsNode` geometry of children that were added to a dirty parent | `buildSemanticsSubtree` computes the geometry of the dirty children before it builds the nodes of the subtree: a widget inserted under a `MaterialApp` with semantics on had a child without geometry yet (a nil rect in `updateSemanticsNodeGeometry`). Regression test `material/semantics_insertion_test.go` | a defect found in step 13.10 |
| `ThemeData.scrollbarTheme`, `progressIndicatorTheme`, `dataTableTheme`, `carouselViewTheme` | `ThemeData.ScrollbarTheme`, `ProgressIndicatorTheme`, `DataTableTheme` and `CarouselViewTheme`; each lerp that Flutter does with `t < 0.5 ? a : b` picks the nearer end | the four themes of step 13.10 |
| Window decorations (Flutter's GTK embedder draws them with GTK) | the choice is made per window, in `internal/platform/linux/wayland/decoration.go`: `DecorationServerSide` when the compositor has `zxdg_decoration_manager_v1`, else `DecorationLibdecor` when `libdecor-0.so.0` loads and starts, else `DecorationApp`; `WindowConfig.Decorations = DecorationsApp` and `Decorated: false` skip it (`DecorationApp`, `DecorationNone`). The platform reports it, with the title, the `wm_capabilities`, and the maximized, fullscreen, active and tiled states, in `ViewMetrics.Window` (`ui.WindowInfo`, read with `View.Window()`). A compositor that answers a request for server-side decorations with client-side ones turns the window into `DecorationApp`. `GLUTTER_DECORATIONS=auto\|app\|libdecor` chooses for the first window (`libdecor` is a test aid: libdecor even where the compositor has its own bars; the error tag of any other value is `DecorationsUnknown`). A libdecor window has no `xdg_surface` and no `xdg_toplevel` of its own (libdecor makes them), so the window goes through `decor.Frame` for the title, the size limits, the states, moves and resizes, and answers a configure with `libdecor_frame_commit`; libdecor's file descriptor is a source of the loop and `Dispatch` runs when it is readable. libdecor's own tags are `LibdecorInitFailed`, `LibdecorDecorateFailed` and `LibdecorDispatchFailed`. Without a plugin libdecor starts with a dummy one and the window has no title bar; Glutter cannot tell | SPEC §8.2, D16 |
| `WindowDecoration` | `NewWindowDecoration(child, WindowDecorationOpts{...})` in package `widgets`: the bar (title, minimize, maximize or restore, close, limited by the capabilities), a drag of it moves the window, a double tap maximizes or restores it, a right click opens the window menu, and strips of 6 pixels along the edges start a resize; it shows the child alone when another decoration draws the title bar. `RunApp` wraps the app in one unless `WindowConfig.Decorations` is `DecorationsApp`. The child keeps its state when the bar appears (a global key), since the compositor says the mode after the first frame. The actions go through `View.PerformWindowAction` and `ui.WindowHost` (the engine) to `platform.Window.PerformWindowAction`, which needs the serial of the last pointer press | the title bar of the GTK embedder is not Flutter API |
| Shadow of `WindowDecoration` | the bar has a one pixel outline and no drop shadow, so `xdg_surface.set_window_geometry` is not used for it (SPEC §8.2 says the shadow and the resize borders sit inside the surface and are excluded with it) | no step of the plan takes the shadow: it needs the geometry of the surface, and the resize borders and the input region with it |
| Decorations on X11 | the window manager draws the title bar of a decorated window and `ViewMetrics.Window` says `DecorationServerSide`; for an app that asked for `DecorationsApp` it is told to draw none (`_MOTIF_WM_HINTS`), the window says `DecorationApp`, and `PerformWindowAction` moves, resizes and opens the window menu through `_NET_WM_MOVERESIZE` and `_GTK_SHOW_WINDOW_MENU` (a window manager that ignores the Motif hints keeps its own bar next to the app's) | step 14.8; the manager does the moving |
| `RegularWindowController` and `DialogWindowController` are abstract classes with factory constructors that ask `WidgetsBinding.instance.windowingOwner`; they throw when windowing is off | `NewRegularWindowController(bc, size, RegularWindowControllerOpts{Constraints, Title, Delegate, Decorations})` and `NewDialogWindowController(bc, size, DialogWindowControllerOpts{..., Parent})` return `(controller, error)`: the owner of the binding reached through the context makes the window. They fail with `WindowingUnsupported` (the platform cannot) or the platform's error, and panic with `WindowSizeViolatesConstraints` in debug builds (Flutter's `assert`); a dialog whose parent was destroyed fails with `WindowParentDestroyed`. Windowing is always on | no singletons (§13); Go returns errors |
| `RegularWindowController.sizedToContent`, `DialogWindowController.sizedToContent` | not ported: a window is created with a size; the windows that fit their content are deferred with the popups, tooltips and satellites | needs the content size to flow back to the platform before the first frame |
| `setFullscreen(fullscreen, {display})`, `isMinimized` | `SetFullscreen(fullscreen)` has no display (the compositor chooses). Wayland does not say whether a window is minimized, so `IsMinimized` is what the controller last asked for, until the window is activated; X11 does (`WindowInfo.Minimized`, from `WM_STATE`), and where the platform has said that a window is minimized, `IsMinimized` is what the platform says | xdg-shell has neither |
| `activate()` | asks the platform; on Wayland `xdg_toplevel` cannot raise a window or take the focus for it, so it asks `xdg_activation_v1` for a token (with the serial of the last input event) and activates the window with it, and the compositor may only mark the window as wanting attention; without the protocol it does nothing. X11 sends `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` | the protocol (step 14.7) |
| a window has no icon and no content type API; Flutter's apps get the icon of the platform's runner | `WindowConfig.Icon`, `WindowConfig.ContentType`, the options `Icon` and `ContentType` of both window controllers, and `SetIcon(icon) error` and `SetContentType(t)` on them (and on `ui.View`): `ui.WindowIcon{Name, Images}` (square RGBA pictures, `ui.WindowIconImage{Size, Scale, RGBA}`; `WindowIconInvalid` for a wrong picture) and `ui.ContentType` (`ContentTypeUnspecified`, `Photo`, `Video`, `Game`). The host side is `ui.WindowAppearanceHost`, which the engine implements with `platform.Window.SetIcon` and `SetContentType`. Wayland uses `xdg_toplevel_icon_v1` and `wp_content_type_v1`; X11 uses `_NET_WM_ICON` for the pictures and has no content type | the desktop's icon and the compositor's hints (SPEC §8.7, §8.11) |
| `wakelock_plus` is a plugin | `desktop.KeepScreenOn(bc, on) error` and `ui.View.KeepScreenOn(on)`, through `ui.IdleInhibitHost` (the engine) and `platform.Window.KeepScreenOn`: `zwp_idle_inhibit_manager_v1` on Wayland; X11 and a host that cannot fail with `IdleInhibitUnavailable` (the D-Bus `org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver` route is not done) | Flutter has no such API; a plugin does it |
| `PointerDeviceKind.stylus` events come from the platform's touch screen | the Wayland backend reports the tools of `zwp_tablet_v2` as stylus, inverted stylus, touch or mouse devices (SPEC §8.11, "Tablets") | the protocol |
| `RegularWindowControllerDelegate` and `DialogWindowControllerDelegate` are mixin classes | interfaces with `OnWindowCloseRequested(controller)` and `OnWindowDestroyed()`, and `RegularWindowControllerDelegateBase` / `DialogWindowControllerDelegateBase` to embed (the default destroys the window). A panic in the delegate is reported as `CallbackPanicked` | Go has no mixins |
| `WindowScope.updateShouldNotify` is `controller != oldWidget.controller`, so its dependents are never told that the title, size or state changed | the scope keeps the state of the window it was made with and notifies when it changes (`updateShouldNotifyDependent` compares the aspects as in Flutter); `RegularWindow` and `DialogWindow` rebuild it when the controller notifies. `WindowScopeOf` / `WindowScopeMaybeOf` return the controller; the aspect getters are `WindowScopeContentSizeOf`, `TitleOf`, `IsActivatedOf`, `IsMinimizedOf`, `IsMaximizedOf`, `IsFullscreenOf` and their `Maybe` forms | the Flutter version does not update |
| the window that the embedder makes has no `WindowScope` | `WindowingOwner.ImplicitWindowController` is the controller of the first window and `RunApp` wraps the app in its `WindowScope` (`WidgetsBinding.WrapWithWindowScope`), so a dialog can name the first window as its parent. The delegate of this controller is not asked to close the window: a request to close it is a request to exit the app (`AppLifecycleListener.OnExitRequested`, §8.7) | `WindowScope.of(context)` is how Flutter's own example names the parent, and the first window needs to be one |
| a dialog with a parent is modal to it | `WindowSpec.HasParent` on a dialog sets `xdg_toplevel.set_parent` and, with `xdg_wm_dialog_v1`, `xdg_dialog_v1.set_modal` (X11: `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR`, `_NET_WM_STATE_MODAL`). The dialogs of a controller close with it, in the owner (no compositor does that). sway has no `xdg_wm_dialog_v1` and shows no sign of the parent, so the relation is checked against the fake compositor | the windowing protocols |
| `WindowManager` renders only the registered windows when there is no `View` above it | it renders its child too, which has to be a `View` or a window widget; otherwise the window of the app would have no content | looks like an oversight in Flutter |
| `ViewCollection` and `ViewAnchor` check in debug builds that no render object ancestor expects the views | the check reports the tag `ViewCollectionInRenderTree` through the error reporter (Flutter's `FlutterError.reportError`) | §14.2 |
| `PlatformDispatcher.requestViewFocusChange`, `onViewFocusChange` and `ViewFocusEvent` | `RequestViewFocusChange(view, state, direction)`, `OnViewFocusChange` and `ui.ViewFocusEvent{ViewID, State, Direction}`; the platform tells the engine about the keyboard focus of a window (`wl_keyboard.enter` and `leave`, XI focus events) with `Handler.OnViewFocus`, the engine queues the event with the input of the next frame, and asks the platform to activate a window when the framework moves the focus into it. Direction is always `ViewFocusDirectionUndefined`, so a window that gets the focus gives it back to the widget that had it | no engine to ask |
| the frames of several windows | one frame of the framework renders every view; the window whose frame is running draws its scene at once, and the others present theirs in their own frames, which `ScheduleFrame` asked for (the engine asks every window, since the framework does not say which of its views changed). A window that asked for no frame is not drawn again. All windows draw through one `gles.Renderer` with one GL context and a surface for each. The self-capture (`GLUTTER_SCREENSHOT`) is of the first window | `PlatformDispatcher.onDrawFrame` has no view |
| `gluttertest` has one view | `Tester.Windows`, `ViewActions`, `ViewScenes`, `FocusRequests`, `SetWindowSize`, `SetWindowInfo`, `RequestWindowClose`, `SetViewFocus` and `SetWindowCreateError` stand in for the platform of the other windows, and `GestureOpts.View` sends a gesture to a view (the gestures that take a finder use the view of the element) | the test platform is a fake engine |
| an optional positional parameter (`CatmullRomCurve(points, [tension])`, `FileImage(file, {scale})`, `Locale(language, [country])`) or a list of values (`LogicalKeySet(k1, [k2, ...])`) | a trailing variadic parameter of that type (`NewCatmullRomCurve(points, tension ...float64)`; at most one value is meaningful for an optional parameter), not an `XOpts` value; the `apicheck` rule `ctor-shape` allows it. The API review of step 18.1 also made `NewRouteInformation`, `NewDrivenScrollActivity`, `NewMultiFrameImageStreamCompleter` and `NewButtonStyleButtonBase` take `opts ...XOpts`, as every constructor does | Go has no optional positional parameters, and a struct for one value is heavy |
| Constructors of Flutter classes with more than 6 required parameters (`ColorScheme` 9, `RawRadio` 8, `SelectionOverlay` 10, `TextSelectionOverlay`, `FixedExtentMetricsSnapshot`, `FixedPageMetrics` 7, `FrameTiming` 7) | they stay public with all required parameters positional: §12.1 asks for a review, and for these the friendlier form is the options struct or `CopyWith` of the object they build; `cmd/glutter-gen/apicheck.allow` lists them | the parameters are all required by Flutter and have no defaults |
| `kElevationToShadow` and `kMaterialEdges` are `const` maps | `ElevationToShadow` and `MaterialEdges` are package variables that hold maps (the only exported variables that are not struct values, the rest of the table values like `Colors` being structs); they are read-only by contract | Go has no constant maps; `apicheck.allow` lists them |
| Enums whose zero value is not `Unspecified` (`ScriptCategory`, `DatePickerMode`, `StretchMode`, `Decorations`, `ScrollPositionAlignmentPolicy`, `DiagonalDragBehavior`, `SemanticsRole` and the other `ui` semantics enums, `WindowKind`, `ColorSpace`, `Assertiveness`, and the enums that are results, events or states such as `StateLifecycle`, `RoutePopDisposition`, `RefreshIndicatorStatus`, `PathVerb`, `DropPhase`, `DayPeriod`) | the zero value is Flutter's default for the enum, so `Unspecified` would mean the same thing, or no options struct takes the enum (it is a result, an event or a state) | §12.3 wants `Unspecified` so that an options struct can tell "not given" from the first value; neither case arises. `apicheck.allow` lists them, with the reason |
| `EditableText.debugDeterministicCursor` is a static variable | `widgets.DebugOptions.DeterministicCursor`, read through the binding in debug builds | no global mutable state (§12.9) |
| `kIsWeb` | `foundation.IsWeb`, a constant set by build tags | named like `DebugMode`; the framework reads it where Flutter reads `kIsWeb` |
| Flutter web draws with CanvasKit or Skwasm (Skia on WebGL 2) | WebGPU through `explicit`, else WebGL 2 through `gles`, else `soft` into a 2D canvas (§9.6) | Glutter's renderers are its own; there is no Skia |
| Flutter web downloads Noto fallback fonts from Google's font CDN | the web has the bundled fonts and the fonts the app loads; a character that none of them has draws as a missing-glyph box (§23.6) | no network access the app did not ask for; a page cannot read the system's fonts |
| Flutter web can render several views into host elements (multi-view) | one view per page, filling the page or one host element (§23.2); a second `NewWindow` fails with `WindowUnsupported` | the 1.1 scope |
| `Picture.toImage` is drawn by the GPU on every renderer | on WebGPU, `Snapshot` draws with `soft` (§9.3) | a WebGPU readback finishes in a later browser task, and `ToImage` returns at once |

### 12.11 One import for apps

Flutter's `material.dart` re-exports `widgets.dart`, which exports all of `animation.dart` and `painting.dart` and the names that its libraries re-export from `foundation`, `rendering`, `gestures`, `services`, `physics`, `scheduler` and `dart:ui`. Glutter gets the same effect with `cmd/glutter-gen reexport` (run it in the root of the module; `go generate ./material` does), which type-checks those packages and writes `material/widgets_reexport_gen.go`:
- type aliases (generic aliases included)
- thin wrapper functions (generic ones too), which the compiler inlines
- the constants and the variables, copied (a read-only value such as `Curves` or `Colors` is a copy; a variable of a basic type is not copied, as a copy would not follow the writes to the original, and the file says which ones)

The packages are re-exported whole (`widgets`, `animation`, `painting`) or by the names that Flutter exports (`DefaultSpecs` in `cmd/glutter-gen/internal/reexport`): a Go identifier belongs to a name when it is that name, or starts with it (optionally after `New`) and goes on with a capital letter or a digit, so that the constructors, the options and the functions of a class come with it. `foundation.Opt`, `Some` and `None` are re-exported too, as the options structs need them. When two packages have an identifier of the same name the first one wins (`widgets`, `animation`, `painting`, then the others), and an identifier that package `material` declares itself is not re-exported (the file lists them). A function whose signature has a type that another package cannot name (an unexported type, or a type of an `internal` package) is left out.

Apps import only `m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"`. The widgets layer itself never imports `material`.

Apps import only `m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"`. The widgets layer itself never imports `material`.

### 12.12 Reference example

This is `examples/counter/main.go` without its header comment and without the code of `GLUTTER_DEV_TAPS` (`InitState` and `devTaps`, which start the counter at that number in debug builds, for the proof screenshot). It differs from the first draft of this section in the names that Glutter's API actually has: `m.Colors.Blue.Primary`, `m.Icons.Add()` (§12.10) and the `TextTheme.HeadlineMedium` field. It also sets the window and turns the debug banner off, so the screenshot shows the page only.

```go
package main

import (
	"log"
	"strconv"

	m "github.com/rm4n0s/glutter/material"
)

func main() {
	err := m.RunApp(NewMyApp(), m.RunAppOpts{
		AppID:  "dev.glutter.counter",
		Window: m.WindowConfig{Title: "Counter", Size: m.Size{Width: 480, Height: 640}},
	})
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatal(err)
	}
}

type MyApp struct{ m.StatelessWidget }

func NewMyApp() *MyApp { return &MyApp{} }

func (a *MyApp) Build(bc m.BuildContext) m.Widget {
	return m.NewMaterialApp(m.MaterialAppOpts{
		Title:                      "Counter",
		Theme:                      m.NewThemeData(m.ThemeDataOpts{ColorSchemeSeed: m.Some(m.Colors.Blue.Primary)}),
		DebugShowCheckedModeBanner: m.Some(false),
		Home:                       NewCounterPage("Counter"),
	})
}

type CounterPage struct {
	m.StatefulWidget
	title string
}

func NewCounterPage(title string, opts ...m.WidgetOpts) *CounterPage {
	return &CounterPage{StatefulWidget: m.NewStatefulWidgetBase(opts...), title: title}
}

func (p *CounterPage) Title() string        { return p.title }
func (p *CounterPage) CreateState() m.State { return &counterPageState{} }

type counterPageState struct {
	m.StateOf[*CounterPage]
	count int
}

func (s *counterPageState) increment() { s.SetState(func() { s.count++ }) }

func (s *counterPageState) Build(bc m.BuildContext) m.Widget {
	return m.NewScaffold(m.ScaffoldOpts{
		AppBar: m.NewAppBar(m.AppBarOpts{Title: m.NewText(s.Widget().Title())}),
		Body: m.NewCenter(
			m.NewColumn([]m.Widget{
				m.NewText("You have pushed the button this many times:"),
				m.NewText(strconv.Itoa(s.count), m.TextOpts{Style: m.ThemeOf(bc).TextTheme.HeadlineMedium}),
			}, m.ColumnOpts{MainAxisAlignment: m.MainAxisAlignmentCenter}),
		),
		FloatingActionButton: m.NewFloatingActionButton(s.increment, m.NewIcon(m.Icons.Add()),
			m.FloatingActionButtonOpts{Tooltip: "Increment"}),
	})
}
```

---

### 12.13 The API check

`go run ./cmd/glutter-gen apicheck -allow cmd/glutter-gen/apicheck.allow` reads the public packages (`foundation` to `material`, `ui`, `desktop` and `gluttertest`) and reports what breaks §12:

| Rule | What it checks |
|---|---|
| `ctor-shape` | an exported `NewX` takes no `XOpts` value except as its trailing variadic parameter (§12.1) |
| `ctor-order` | `child`, `children`, `sliver` and `slivers` are the last positional parameters (§12.2) |
| `ctor-size` | a constructor has at most 6 required parameters (§12.1) |
| `doc-ref` | the doc comment of an exported type or function ends with a `Flutter:` (`Flutter: no counterpart` included) or `Glutter-specific:` paragraph |
| `enum-zero` | an integer type declared as `T = iota, ...` starts with `<T>Unspecified` (§12.3); `1 << iota` flag types are not enums in this sense |
| `mutable-var` | an exported package variable is not a map, slice, function, channel or basic value, and no statement of its package assigns to it (§ "No global mutable state" of CLAUDE.md) |

`cmd/glutter-gen/apicheck.allow` lists the violations that §12.10 accepts, one `rule pkg.Name` per line with the reason as a comment; a line that no longer matches fails the check. `TestTheModuleKeepsTheConventions` in `cmd/glutter-gen/internal/apicheck` runs it on the module, so `go test ./...` keeps the API in line.

## 13. Threading and lifecycle

- `m.RunApp(app Widget, opts ...RunAppOpts) error` must run on the main goroutine (§8.4); `RunWidget` is the same without the default `View`. It returns when the last window closes or after `SystemNavigatorPop`. Environment failures come back as tagged errors (§14.1).
- `RunAppOpts` include:
  - `Assets fs.FS` for the `AssetBundle`
  - `AppID`, `Window` (the first window's config)
  - `Backend` (overrides `GLUTTER_BACKEND`) and `Renderer` (overrides `GLUTTER_RENDERER`, §9.6)
  - `ErrorReporter` and `ErrorWidgetBuilder` (§14.2)
  - `Logger *slog.Logger`
  - `Debug *DebugOptions` (§14.3)
- The UI thread owns the element tree, render tree, `ChangeNotifier`s, controllers and GL. From other goroutines, only these are safe:
  - `State.SetState`
  - `WidgetsBindingOf(bc).Post(fn)`
  - the `desktop` service calls (they take a `context.Context` and deliver results with `Post`)
- `debug` builds detect calls made from the wrong goroutine on the main framework entry points and report them with tag `WrongThread`.
- App lifecycle maps to `AppLifecycleState`:
  - `Resumed`: focused
  - `Inactive`: visible but unfocused
  - `Hidden`: minimized or all windows hidden
  - `Detached`: shutting down
  - `Paused` is not used on desktop, as in Flutter's desktop embedders.
- On the web the UI thread is the main goroutine too; §23.3 says how the browser's callbacks reach it, and the one rule that keeps them from deadlocking.

## 14. Errors and diagnostics

### 14.1 Environment errors

These are returned from `RunApp`, image/font loading and `desktop` calls, always with tags from `rm4n0s/errors`. The initial tags are:
- `LibraryLoadFailed`, `NoDisplay`, `BackendUnknown`, `BackendUnavailable`, `WaylandConnectFailed`, `WaylandProtocolMissing`, `WaylandProtocolError`, `X11OpenDisplayFailed`, `IdleInhibitUnavailable`
- `EGLLoadFailed`, `EGLInitFailed`, `EGLNoConfig`, `GLES3Unsupported`, `ShaderCompileFailed`
- `AssetNotFound`, `ImageDecodeFailed`, `FontLoadFailed`
- `DBusConnectFailed`, `TrayUnavailable`, `NotificationsUnavailable`, `FileChooserUnavailable`, `OpenURIUnavailable`
- `RendererUnknown`, `RendererUnavailable`, `VulkanLoadFailed`, `VulkanUnsupported`, `VulkanNoDevice`, `VulkanSurfaceFailed`, `VulkanSwapchainFailed`, `VulkanDeviceLost`, `GPUOutOfMemory`, `GPUDeviceLost`, `ShaderModuleInvalid` (1.1, §9.3–9.6)
- `WebGPUUnavailable`, `WebGPUDeviceFailed`, `WebGL2Unavailable`, `HostElementNotFound`, `WindowUnsupported` (1.1, §23)
- `MultipleOptions`, `WrongThread`

Every tag has a route test.

### 14.2 Programmer errors and panics in app code (D15)

Glutter follows Flutter. Flutter reports failed asserts and exceptions through `FlutterError.onError`, replaces the failing subtree with `ErrorWidget`, and keeps the app running.

- **Signaling**: `Build`, layout, paint and callbacks never return errors. Bugs panic: Go runtime panics, and failed debug checks, which panic with a tagged `*errors.Error` (e.g. `SetStateAfterDispose`).
- **Recovery is done by the library.** App code never writes `recover()`. The framework recovers at the same points where Flutter catches exceptions:

  | Recovery point | Flutter equivalent |
  |---|---|
  | `Build` of every element | `ComponentElement.performRebuild` |
  | layout and paint of every render object | `RenderObject._reportException` |
  | gesture callbacks | `GestureRecognizer.invokeCallback` |
  | `Listenable` and animation listeners | `ChangeNotifier.notifyListeners` |
  | scheduler frame/post-frame callbacks and timers | `SchedulerBinding` |
  | image and font stream listeners | `ImageStreamCompleter` |

- **Reporting**: each recovered panic becomes a tagged error (`BuildPanicked`, `LayoutPanicked`, `PaintPanicked`, `CallbackPanicked`) carrying the panic value, the stack and the element path. A panic that is already a tagged `*errors.Error` (a failed debug check) is reported with its own tag. It goes to `RunAppOpts.ErrorReporter func(error)`, which the binding passes to every part of the framework (§12.10). A part that has no reporter does not recover. The default reporter logs `appErr.ToJson()` through the app's `slog.Logger`. An app can forward errors to its own service, or call `os.Exit` to crash instead.
- **Display**: the failing subtree is replaced by `RunAppOpts.ErrorWidgetBuilder func(error) Widget`. The default is Flutter's: a red box with the message in debug builds, a plain gray box in release builds.
- **Goroutines**: Go can't catch a panic in a goroutine started with a bare `go` statement, so it crashes the program. Use `State.Go(func(ctx context.Context))`, which runs the function with the state's `Lifetime()` context under the same recovery and reporting.
- **Tests**: `gluttertest` fails a test on any reported error unless the test takes it with `tester.TakeError()`, Flutter's `takeException()`.
- **Constructors** never return errors or error widgets (D1).

### 14.3 Debugging aids

- Flags in `rendering`/`widgets`: `DebugPaintSizeEnabled`, `DebugPaintBaselinesEnabled`, `DebugPaintLayerBordersEnabled`, `DebugRepaintRainbowEnabled`, `DebugPrintRebuildDirtyWidgets`, `DebugPrintLayouts`, `DebugProfileBuildsEnabled`. These are fields of a `DebugOptions` value passed through `RunAppOpts`, not global variables.
- `DebugDumpApp`, `DebugDumpRenderTree`, `DebugDumpLayerTree`, `DebugDumpSemanticsTree`, `DebugDumpFocusTree`.
- A performance overlay (frame build and raster time graphs): `MaterialApp.ShowPerformanceOverlay` or the `PerformanceOverlay` widget, drawn from the frame timings that the engine reports.
- `runtime/trace` regions around the frame phases.

---

## 15. Performance targets

These are measured on a 2020 laptop with an Intel Iris Xe or similar GPU and Mesa, at 1920×1080 and a device pixel ratio of 1.25.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Idle CPU (no animation, no input) | ~0%: no frames scheduled, no timers armed |
| Frame rate (gallery screen, ~500 visible widgets, one animation) | the display's refresh rate with no dropped frames |
| UI thread CPU per frame, animation-only frame (no rebuild) | < 2 ms |
| UI thread CPU per frame, full rebuild of the gallery screen | < 8 ms |
| Steady-state allocations, animation-only frame | ≤ 1 KB per frame |
| Cold start to first frame | < 150 ms (fonts cached) |
| Resident memory, counter example | < 60 MB |

Benchmarks (`go test -bench`) in `rendering`, `widgets`, `examples/gallery` and `internal/gpu/soft`, and `scripts/perf.sh`, track these numbers; the targets are for release builds (`-tags glutter_release`). `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` has the machine, the numbers and the exceptions. Regressions above 10% need a justification.

**Vulkan** (1.1) meets the same targets on the same machine, and no benchmark is more than 10% slower with Vulkan than with GLES.

**Web** (1.1), measured in Chromium (WebGPU) and Firefox (WebGL 2) on the same machine, release builds made by `glutter-web build -release`:

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Idle CPU | ~0%: no `requestAnimationFrame` asked for while nothing is scheduled |
| Frame rate (gallery screen, one animation) | the display's refresh rate with no dropped frames |
| UI thread CPU per frame, animation-only frame | < 4 ms |
| UI thread CPU per frame, full rebuild of the gallery screen | < 16 ms |
| Size of `app.wasm.gz`, counter / gallery | < 4 MB / < 5 MB (on 2026-10-21, without the web platform and renderers: 2.3 MB / 2.4 MB) |
| Start to first frame, `app.wasm` in the browser's cache | < 1 s |

## 16. Build and runtime requirements

**Building an app**: Go 1.27+ and nothing else. `CGO_ENABLED=0 go build` produces a binary that loads the system libraries at runtime. It's dynamically linked against glibc's loader, which is how purego reaches `dlopen`. Cross-compiling for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 from any OS works.

**Building for the web** (1.1): `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build`, or `go run ./cmd/glutter-web build` for a directory a static web server can serve (§23.1). Only the standard Go toolchain is supported (D18).

**Contributing**: Go 1.27+ plus a C compiler, **only** because `go test -race` requires cgo. When cgo is enabled, purego uses it transparently, so the same code is tested both ways (§17). No `-devel` packages, `wayland-scanner` or `wayland-protocols` are needed.

**Runtime**:
- **Required**:
  - a Wayland compositor with `xdg_wm_base`, or an X server
  - an EGL driver with OpenGL ES 3.0 (Mesa ≥ 20, NVIDIA through glvnd, or llvmpipe)
  - the runtime libraries in §8.1. They're installed on every Linux desktop (Wayland compositors and Xwayland depend on them), and a missing one is reported as a tagged error.
- **Optional**:
  - a D-Bus session bus, which every desktop session has; all the services below use it
  - xdg-desktop-portal plus a backend, for file dialogs, settings and open URI
  - a StatusNotifierItem host, for the tray (KDE, XFCE, Cinnamon, and GNOME with the AppIndicator extension)
  - a notification daemon
  - at-spi2-core, for accessibility
  - a Vulkan driver (§9.4); without one, GLES is used (§9.6)

**Runtime on the web** (1.1): a browser with WebAssembly and WebGL 2, which every current browser has. WebGPU is used where the browser has it (Chromium-based browsers, Safari, and Firefox where it ships WebGPU).

**Contributing to 1.1** also needs: Node.js (the wasm unit tests, §17), Chromium and Firefox (the browser tests), Mesa's lavapipe and `vulkan-validation-layers` (the Vulkan display tests), and `naga` (`cargo install naga-cli --locked`), only to change a WGSL shader (D20).

## 17. Testing strategy

- **Unit tests** are table-driven and sit next to the code (CLAUDE.md).
- **Widget tests** (`gluttertest`):
  - `t := gluttertest.New(tb)`, then `PumpWidget`, `Pump(d)`, `PumpAndSettle`, `Tap`, `LongPress`, `Drag`, `Fling`, `Hover`, `Scroll`, `EnterText`, `SendKeyEvent`, `SendKeyDownEvent`/`UpEvent`, `PumpFrames`
  - finders: `ByText`, `ByKey`, `ByType[T]`, `ByIcon`, `ByTooltip`, `BySemanticsLabel`, `Descendant`, `Ancestor`
  - assertions are plain functions returning errors for `t.Error` (`FindsOneWidget`, `FindsNothing`, ...); `MatchesGolden(name)` compares the view with a golden
  - a fake clock drives tickers and timers
  - the `fake` platform scripts windows, input and settings
- **Golden tests** compare against PNGs in `testdata/goldens/`, rendered by `internal/gpu/soft`. `-update-goldens` rewrites them.
- **Ported Flutter tests**: for each widget and render object, the relevant cases from Flutter's `packages/flutter/test` are ported as table tests. This is the main way to check fidelity.
- **Platform tests** (`-tags glutter_display`) open real Wayland/X11 windows and GL contexts (they can run under a headless compositor such as `weston --backend=headless` or `Xvfb`), and compare `gles` against `soft`.
- **Wayland compatibility matrix.**
  - Every milestone that touches the platform is checked against:
    - compositors: GNOME/Mutter, KDE/KWin, Sway, Hyprland, Weston and COSMIC
    - drivers: Mesa (Intel, AMD), NVIDIA proprietary, and llvmpipe
    - scales: 100%, 125%, 150% and 200%
    - a two-monitor setup with mixed scales
  - The automated part runs under headless compositors (`weston --backend=headless`, `sway` with `WLR_BACKENDS=headless`, `mutter --headless`, `kwin_wayland --virtual`).
  - The rest is a manual checklist in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` covering every item in §8.11.
- **D-Bus services** are tested against an in-process fake bus that records method calls and emits signals. Real-desktop checks are listed per milestone in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md`.
  - The tests of an app that uses package `desktop` call `tester.FakeDesktop()` (`gluttertest`): a notification service, a StatusNotifierWatcher and a file chooser portal on the fake bus. The test reads `Notifications()`, `TrayMenu()` and `TrayProperty`, and acts like the user with `ClickTrayMenuItem`, `ActivateTray`, `InvokeNotificationAction`, `CloseNotification`, `AnswerFileChooser` and `CancelFileChooser`; `WaitFor` runs the UI thread's posted functions while the app's goroutines work.
  - What the fake bus cannot show is tested against a real `dbus-daemon` that a test starts (it is skipped when the daemon is not installed): the types that godbus gives and takes (the layout `(ia{sv}av)`, the pixmaps, the errors of a missing service, which are values and not pointers) and the life of the connection.
- **Both build modes**:
  - `go test -race ./...` runs with cgo (purego uses the real cgo runtime)
  - `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./...` runs purego's cgo-free path, which users get
  - both must pass
- **C struct layout tests** for every Go mirror of a C struct (§8.1 rule 3).
- **Fuzz tests** cover the X11/Wayland event decoders, the XKB compose handling, the paragraph layout, and the image and font loaders.
- **Vulkan** display tests (`glutter_display`) run under headless sway, and under Xvfb for X11, with Mesa's lavapipe (`VK_DRIVER_FILES` pointing at `lvp_icd.*.json`) and with validation on (§9.4); a validation error fails the test. Every golden scene is compared against `soft`, as for `gles`.
- **Web unit tests**: `scripts/test-wasm.sh` runs the tests of every package that has no OS tag with `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go test` under Node (Go's `go_js_wasm_exec`). It runs them with a minimal environment, because Go's wasm loader limits the size of the environment.
- **Browser tests** (`-tags glutter_browser`): `internal/webtest` builds a wasm test program, serves it with `net/http/httptest`, and starts a headless browser with a temporary profile: Chromium for WebGPU and WebGL 2, Firefox for WebGL 2 (and WebGPU where it has it). The page posts its results and its read-back PNGs to the server, and the test compares them with the `soft` goldens. No WebDriver or DevTools library is used. Every browser process is killed when its test ends.

## 18. Milestones

Each milestone is broken into steps in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` (written after this spec is approved) and follows the workflow in CLAUDE.md.

| # | Milestone | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | Skeleton: `go.mod`, package layout, `cmd/glutter-gen` stub, `reference/` fetch script, `third_party/flutter/LICENSE` | `go build ./...` and all four checks pass |
| M1 | Platform core: purego library loaders and C struct mirrors, Wayland binding generator, Wayland and X11 window, event loop, EGL, GLES clear, frame callbacks, pointer and keyboard (xkb), fake platform | a window opens and closes and logs input on both backends |
| M2 | `ui` engine + software renderer: geometry, Path, Paint, Canvas, Picture, SceneBuilder, analytic shapes, shadows, gradients, clips, layers, golden harness | goldens for every `Canvas` operation |
| M3 | GLES renderer to parity with `soft`, plus atlases and batching | `gles` matches the `soft` goldens within tolerance |
| M4 | Text: font collection, fontscan, Roboto embedded, Paragraph with every query, glyph atlas | text goldens, including bidi, ellipsis and decorations |
| M5 | foundation, scheduler, physics, gestures (hit testing, arena, every recognizer) | ported Flutter gesture tests pass |
| M6 | rendering core: RenderObject, RenderBox, PipelineOwner, layers, compositing, every box render object | ported Flutter rendering tests pass |
| M7 | widgets core: Element tree, State, keys, GlobalKey, InheritedWidget family, basic widgets, MediaQuery, `RunApp`, `gluttertest` | counter example runs on Linux |
| M8 | animation library, implicit and explicit animations, transitions, Hero | ported animation tests pass |
| M9 | scrolling: Scrollable, physics, ListView/GridView/CustomScrollView, slivers, PageView, NestedScrollView, scrollbars, reorderable lists | ported scrolling tests pass |
| M10 | navigation and focus: Navigator (both APIs), Router, Overlay, OverlayPortal, focus system, Shortcuts/Actions | ported tests pass |
| M11 | text editing: EditableText, selection, Linux shortcuts, undo, IME (text-input-v3 on Wayland; IBus/Fcitx5 over D-Bus on X11), clipboard, SelectableRegion | editing works with IBus and Fcitx5 on both backends |
| M12 | Material foundation: ThemeData, ColorScheme (+ HCT port), Typography, Material, Ink, icons, localizations | theme goldens |
| M13 | Material components, in batches: app structure → actions → inputs → dialogs and pickers → display | gallery example covers §11.2 |
| M14 | desktop windowing: multiple windows, dialog windows, decorations (server-side, libdecor, app-drawn), cursor shapes, fractional scale, settings, drag and drop, every item in §8.11 | multiwindow example; drag and drop with Files/Dolphin; the Wayland matrix (§17) passes |
| M15 | D-Bus services: tray, notifications, file dialogs, open URI | tray example on KDE and GNOME+AppIndicator |
| M16 | accessibility: semantics → AT-SPI2 | gallery usable with Orca |
| M17 | effects and performance: backdrop filter, advanced blend modes, raster cache, performance overlay, benchmarks meeting §15 | targets met |
| M18 | 1.0: documentation, examples, API review against §12 | release |
| M19 | Portability groundwork (1.1): per-OS backend files, the js/wasm build, renderer selection, WGSL toolchain, the explicit device interface | `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build ./...` passes; the WGSL shaders compile to SPIR-V |
| M20 | Vulkan renderer, at parity with `soft`, default on Linux with GLES as the fallback | `vulkan` matches the `soft` goldens; `auto` uses Vulkan on Mesa and NVIDIA and GLES without Vulkan |
| M21 | Web: platform, input, text input, WebGL 2, WebGPU, fallback, `glutter-web`, examples | the examples run in Chromium (WebGPU) and Firefox (WebGL 2) and match the goldens; release 1.1 |

## 19. Examples (`examples/`)

`counter`, `layout` (Row/Column/Stack/Wrap), `animations`, `scrolling` (lists, grids, slivers), `navigation`, `editor` (text editing, clipboard, IME), `gallery` (all Material components, light/dark), `multiwindow`, `tray` (tray + notifications + file dialog), `custompaint`, `todo` (a DataTable of tasks with Edit and Delete buttons in each row).

On the web (1.1) every example except `multiwindow` and `tray` builds and runs (§23).

For 1.1 the checkout also holds Flutter's web engine, `engine/src/flutter/lib/web_ui`, at the same commit: its input, keyboard and text-editing code is ported for §23 (Flutter's BSD-3-Clause license covers it).

## 21. Open decisions

None at the moment. New ones are added here with a recommendation and moved to §4 once decided.

## 22. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Without cgo, purego imitates the cgo runtime (`internal/fakecgo`), which depends on Go runtime internals and can break with a new Go release | Pin the purego version. Run both test modes (§17) on every new Go release before raising the minimum Go version. Ebiten depends on the same mechanism, so breakage is found and fixed upstream quickly |
| A wrong Go mirror of a C struct corrupts memory silently | layout tests (§8.1 rule 3); fuzz tests on event decoding |
| The C→Go callback limit (~2,000 per process) | callbacks are created once at startup and dispatch by user data (§8.1 rule 2); a test counts them |
| C variadic APIs can't be called (XIM, some Xlib calls) | non-variadic variants; IME over D-Bus on X11 |
| purego callbacks can't take structs by value | none of the libraries used need that (checked per library in M1) |
| libdecor's GTK plugin loads GTK 3 into the process (more memory, slower first window) | load it only on compositors without server-side decorations; `DecorationsApp` skips it; measure against §15 in M14 |
| Vulkan drivers differ more than GLES drivers (window-system integration, present modes, synchronization) | validation on in the display tests; `auto` falls back to GLES on any failure before the first frame (§9.6); `GLUTTER_RENDERER=gles` is the user's way out |
| Each call from Go into JavaScript costs about a microsecond | a frame's data goes to the GPU in few large writes (§9.5); `gles` already skips repeated GL state. Measured in step 21.7; if it is still too slow, a frame's GL calls are encoded into one buffer that a small JavaScript function runs |
| Some browsers have no WebGPU (Firefox on Linux, older Safari) | the WebGL 2 fallback, tested in Firefox |
| A browser callback that waits for the UI goroutine deadlocks if the UI goroutine waits for a promise | the rule in §23.3; a browser test sends keys while a clipboard read is pending |
| Go's wasm binaries are large | `-ldflags=-s -w`, gzip made by `glutter-web`, and the size targets in §15 |

## 23. Web (`GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm`, 1.1)

### 23.1 Build and packaging

- Apps build with the standard Go toolchain: `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build -o app.wasm`. TinyGo is not supported (D18).
- `go run ./cmd/glutter-web build [-o dir] [-release] <package>` makes a directory that any static web server can serve:
  - `index.html`: the loader, and `globalThis.glutterConfig` (§23.2)
  - `wasm_exec.js`, copied from `$(go env GOROOT)/lib/wasm` (it must match the Go that built the app)
  - `app.wasm` and `app.wasm.gz`

  `-release` builds with `-tags glutter_release -ldflags=-s -w`.
- `glutter-web serve [-addr :8080] <package>` builds and serves the app, with the type `application/wasm`, and sends the gzip file to a browser that accepts it.
- `glutter-web shot -browser chromium|firefox [-renderer r] -o file.png <package>` saves a screenshot of the app (proof method D, §23.8).
- The loader fetches `app.wasm` with `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming`. It copies the query parameters of the page's URL whose names start with `GLUTTER_` into the Go program's environment, so `?GLUTTER_RENDERER=webgl` works as the environment variable does on Linux. Without WebAssembly it shows a message instead.

### 23.2 Page and view

Port from `lib/web_ui/lib/src/engine/view_embedder/` (dimensions, full-page and custom-element embedding).

- One view per page. `globalThis.glutterConfig.hostElement` (a CSS selector, set in `index.html`) names the element the view fills; without it the view fills the page. A selector that matches nothing is `HostElementNotFound`.
- The platform puts a `<canvas>` and the hidden text field (§23.5) into the host. For the full page, the body has no margin and no scroll bars, and the canvas fills the viewport. The canvas has `touch-action: none`.
- The physical size comes from a `ResizeObserver` with `devicePixelContentBoxSize` (exact device pixels), else from `round(CSS size × devicePixelRatio)`. A change of `devicePixelRatio` (zoom, another monitor) is seen through `matchMedia("(resolution: <n>dppx)")`. The canvas's `width` and `height` are set to the physical size before a frame of that size is drawn.
- `WindowConfig.Title` sets `document.title`. Size, size constraints and window states are ignored, except fullscreen (`requestFullscreen` on the host, which the browser allows only during a user action). A second `NewWindow` is `WindowUnsupported`.
- `Displays()` is one display from `screen`, with a refresh rate of 60.
- `TargetPlatform` is the browser's OS, found as Flutter web does (port `lib/web_ui/lib/src/engine/browser_detection.dart` and `packages/flutter/lib/src/foundation/_platform_web.dart`). `foundation.IsWeb` is true.

### 23.3 Event loop and threading

- The UI thread is the main goroutine. `RunApp` calls `Platform.Run`, which waits on the platform's queue; `Post` adds to the queue and wakes it. `RunApp` does not return on the web, unless starting fails; `SystemNavigatorPop` and `RequestAppExit` do nothing, because a page cannot close itself.
- The browser's callbacks (`js.FuncOf`) are made once, at startup, and kept for the life of the app, as §8.1 rule 2 does for C. A callback copies what it needs out of the event into Go values (a JavaScript event is only valid during its callback) and queues it. Go's wasm runtime runs every goroutine that is ready before a callback returns to the browser, so the UI goroutine handles the event in the same browser task.
- Frames: `RequestFrame` asks for one `requestAnimationFrame`, and its timestamp is the frame's time. Nothing is asked for while no frame is scheduled. A hidden page gets no animation frames; the app is then `Hidden`.
- The callbacks that need an answer wait for the UI goroutine. `keydown` and `keyup` call `preventDefault` when the framework handled the key, as Flutter web does; `wheel` calls it when a scrollable took the scroll. That is safe only because of this rule: **once `Run` has installed the event listeners, the UI goroutine never waits for a promise.** A promise (a clipboard read, `mapAsync`, a new WebGPU device after a loss) continues in a `then` callback that posts to the UI goroutine. What startup awaits (`requestAdapter`, `requestDevice`) it awaits before the listeners are installed.
- Wasm runs one goroutine at a time. `SetState` and `Post` from other goroutines work as on Linux, and Go's timers become `setTimeout` calls.

### 23.4 Input

Port from `lib/web_ui/lib/src/engine/pointer_binding.dart` (and `pointer_converter.dart`), `keyboard_binding.dart` and `mouse/cursor.dart`, or the files that hold that code at the pinned commit.

- Pointer events (`pointerdown`, `pointermove`, `pointerup`, `pointercancel`, with `setPointerCapture` on down) become `PointerData` of kind mouse, touch or stylus, with buttons, pressure and tilt. Several touches are told apart by `pointerId`.
- `wheel` becomes scroll deltas, converted from lines and pages to pixels as Flutter web converts them. The listener is not passive, so `preventDefault` can keep the page from scrolling (§23.3).
- `keydown` and `keyup` on the focused element become `KeyData`: the physical key from `event.code`, the logical key from `event.key`, through tables that `glutter-gen keys` generates from Flutter's key data (the web names). Modifier and lock keys get the synthesized events Flutter web sends.
- `MouseCursor` becomes CSS `cursor` names, mapped as Flutter web maps them.
- Focus: the host is focusable (`tabindex`). `focus`/`blur` make the app `Resumed`/`Inactive`, and `visibilitychange` makes it `Hidden`.
- The browser's context menu: port `services.BrowserContextMenu` (enabled by default, as in Flutter; an app can disable it).
- Drag and drop is not in 1.1: files dropped on the page are ignored.

### 23.5 Text input, IME and clipboard

Port from `lib/web_ui/lib/src/engine/text_editing/` (`text_editing.dart`, `composition_aware_mixin.dart`, `input_type.dart`, `input_action.dart`) and `clipboard.dart`.

- A transparent text field (`<textarea>` for multiline fields, `<input>` for single-line ones) is placed at the caret rectangle that `SetTextInput` gives, with the field's font size, so the IME's candidate window opens at the caret. It holds the editing state (text, selection, composing range), and its `beforeinput`, `input`, `compositionstart`, `compositionupdate`, `compositionend` and `selectionchange` events become `TextInputEvent`s. It has the focus while a text field is focused and gives it back to the host on blur.
- Being a real text field, it also brings up the virtual keyboard on phones and tablets, set up through `inputmode` and `enterkeyhint` from `TextInputType` and `TextInputAction`.
- Clipboard: copy and cut through `navigator.clipboard.writeText` (asynchronous; the result is posted to the UI goroutine). Paste through the `paste` event of the text field when the user presses the paste shortcut (no permission needed), and `navigator.clipboard.readText` when the app pastes by itself (the browser may ask the user). Only text is supported on the web; other kinds fail as the platform's `Clipboard` documents. There is no primary selection.

### 23.6 Fonts, assets, images and settings

- There are no system fonts. The text engine gets the bundled fonts (`material/fonts`) and the fonts the app loads (`ui.LoadFontFromList`, `FontLoader`). `fontscan` and fontconfig are not used. A character with no font draws as a missing-glyph box (§12.10).
- Assets come from `RunAppOpts.Assets` (usually an `embed.FS`), as on Linux. Network images go through `net/http`, which uses `fetch` on js/wasm, so the browser's CORS rules apply.
- Settings: `prefers-color-scheme` gives the brightness, `prefers-reduced-motion` turns animations off, `prefers-contrast: more` gives high contrast, `navigator.languages` gives the locales, and the text scale is 1. Changes arrive through `matchMedia` listeners and `languagechange`.

### 23.7 Services

- `desktop.OpenURI` opens the URI with `window.open(uri, "_blank", "noopener")`, after the same validation as on Linux.
- `desktop.ShowNotification` uses the Notifications API. It asks for permission once; if it is refused, `NotificationsUnavailable`.
- The tray and the file dialogs return `TrayUnavailable` and `FileChooserUnavailable`. D-Bus is never tried on the web.
- Accessibility (an ARIA tree from the semantics tree) is not in 1.1. `Services().Accessibility()` is nil.

### 23.8 Testing and proof

- Unit tests run under Node (`scripts/test-wasm.sh`), and browser tests under the `glutter_browser` tag (§17).
- Browsers run headless with a temporary profile: Chromium with `--headless=new` and the flags that turn WebGPU on under Linux (kept in `internal/webtest`, with the reason for each), and Firefox with `--headless`.
- **Proof method D**: `glutter-web shot` builds the app, serves it, and opens it with `GLUTTER_SCREENSHOT=1` in the URL. After the first idle frame (as in step 7.4) the engine reads its frame back and posts a PNG to `/__glutter/screenshot`. The tool saves the PNG and kills the browser.
- `examples/examples_test.go` also builds every web example for js/wasm.
